## 1. Specific goal and optimization objective

**Build and improve a responsive Balatro advisor that can guide and execute one
recommended action per user click throughout every challenge, so the user can
finish all twenty challenges and reduce the grind toward 100% achievements.**

The requested competence milestones apply to **each challenge individually**:
first 50% run wins, then 75%. An average across easy and hard challenges cannot
satisfy those targets. Neither milestone is currently established. The user
accepted rough, low-confidence estimates for development updates instead of
requiring large campaigns before every useful installation.

The ultimate objective is **minimum expected real time to complete all remaining
challenges**, accounting for wins, losses, retries, opening-search time, action
animations, number of hands, shop decisions, and recommendation latency. The
user explicitly prefers an 80% policy if its runs are much faster than a 90%
policy. Win probability is therefore a competence constraint and a major input
to the objective, not the sole optimization target.

For a stationary retry policy with independent fresh attempts:

`expected time to first win = mean attempt duration / probability of a win`

Equivalently:

`mean winning duration + ((1-p)/p) * mean losing duration`

Use both winning and losing durations. Successful-run speed alone rewards
policies that quietly lose many long attempts. For all remaining challenges,
sum their expected completion times; do not let improvements on easy challenges
hide a policy that rarely completes a hard challenge. This objective is **not
yet implemented as a calibrated policy evaluator**. Some current tie-breaks
favor fewer hands/actions, but general time-aware skip routing is still absent.

### Latest ideas to carry forward

The user specifically wants consideration of:

1. **Cumulative Joker progression**, including choosing actions that grow a
   Joker now to make later blinds easier; Yorick was the example.
2. **Simulations to update and calibrate current decisions**, rather than relying
   indefinitely on guessed strategic values.
3. **Evolutionary competition among parameter sets** for values that are not
   exactly dictated by game rules. The user's “cells” can be interpreted as a
   population of candidate coefficient vectors/policies.
4. **Faster, smoother recommendation calculation**, not only stronger decisions.
5. **Stop searching once a legal hand is known to win the current blind**, with
   only cheap, obvious conservation or lasting deck-development alternatives.
   The user reports an obvious Four of a Kind build still doing roughly
   130,000 score comparisons.
6. **Preserve the source of recurring consumable generation.** The user reports
   two Perkeos with a useful Strength Tarot, yet advice to spend the last
   Strength instead of retaining it for future copies and Four/Five of a Kind
   deck development. These are user-reported counterexamples, not reproduced
   fixtures or measured new improvements. See §7 and §9 for acceptance criteria.
7. **Target upgrades according to the established deck plan.** The user reports
   upgrades aimed at an Ace despite a deck with many Kings pursuing Five of a
   Kind. Build awareness should make investment in the Kings the default,
   instead of repeatedly favoring an incidental non-King's immediate score.
   This is another user-reported case to reproduce, not an already fixed bug.
   **Latest clarification:** five Kings plus two Queens should prompt Strength
   on the Queens to develop the Kings build even when the current blind clears
   with certainty either way. Immediate win-probability gain is not required
   for useful long-term investment; reconcile this with the cheap-clear fast
   path and Perkeo source retention rather than suppressing all consumable use.
8. **Generalize Perkeo planning to the whole consumable inventory.** The user
   reports purchases such as Hierophant diluting a sole preferred Strength's
   copying chance. Account for displaced future copies, changing proportions
   as Negative copies accumulate, and alternative Planet/Observatory engines.
   Do not implement a named-card blacklist or treat every additional consumable
   as independently beneficial. See the inventory-composition design in §7.
9. **Spend short-term resources for worthwhile long-run development when
   safely ahead.** The user wants extra played hands to find a Death copying
   target and more discarded cards to grow Yorick, even at the cost of current
   income or hand resources. A strong early position should increase willingness
   to make those investments when there is a useful future payoff. The goal is
   faster, more reliable overall completion, not maximum end-of-blind income.
10. **Choose Burnt Joker's first-discard upgrade dynamically.** The best hand
    to level depends on the deck, Jokers, levels, resources, bosses and future
    plan; do not always pick the most-used, highest-level or biggest hand.
11. **Investigate a fast, efficient simulator** for finding stronger policies,
    the most common failure modes and performance bottlenecks, as well as
    calibration. Preserve per-challenge reporting and distinguish development
    evidence from qualified win rates; the concrete plan is in §8.

These directions can preserve deterministic live inference: offline search can
tune coefficients, and the shipped policy can remain repeatable and lightweight.
No evolutionary optimizer, coefficient registry, trained model, or new
simulation campaign was implemented during this documentation turn. A neural
model/GPU training phase remains a separate, previously deferred direction;
the user has an RTX 5090 FE, but no GPU work has been started. Do not interpret
the new calibration proposal as a reason to immediately launch an expensive
neural-training run.

## 4. How the current advisor works

### 4.1 Data capture and repeatability

The live mod reads internal Lua game objects, not pixels. `snapshot.capture`
copies cards, full relevant ability tables, current money/resources, boss and
challenge fields, hand levels/history, consumable usage and actual eligible
shop pool metadata. Stable card identities and order are retained. The remaining
deck is sorted by identity for sampling so the ordinary advisor does not inspect
the actual next-draw order. Face-down cards still have internal identities; the
advisor uses those and discloses that it can reveal hidden information.

`fingerprint` canonically serializes snapshot data. Hover-only Blueprint tooltip
caches are stripped so hovering does not change the decision. A changed state
cancels stale work; an unchanged state keeps the exact completed result.
Internal sampling is deterministic and separate from the game RNG. Advice does
not advance run RNG, use live speculative callbacks, or change your cards.

“Deterministic” does not mean “no probabilities.” It means fixed inputs produce
the same answer. Random draws are sampled reproducibly; random scoring effects
often use averages. No live learning happens after a win or loss.

### 4.2 Scoring model versus original engine

`scoring.lua` is a manually implemented detached model, not the complete game.
It classifies hands, identifies scoring cards and models many chip/Mult/XMult,
held-card, enhancement, seal, edition, suit/rank, copy/retrigger and Joker-order
effects, plus challenge and boss restrictions. It reads the current level and
counters rather than assuming base cards or level-one hands.

`score` can return uncertainty/warnings and transition information. `after_play`
carries supported deterministic consequences into future states. Unknown or
unsupported transitions should fail explicitly; do not silently claim an exact
future state just because the immediate score was computable.

The separate source-engine harness runs original code read from `Balatro.exe`
inside its Lua DLL. It is the mechanics oracle for focused tests. Its full-run
adapter is still experimental; it cannot yet justify calibrated win-rate claims.

### 4.3 Current hand search

At a hand decision, `search.run` considers legal one-to-five-card subsets.
An eight-card hand has 218 such subsets. Selection limits, forced cards and
boss restrictions matter. Up to twenty held cards can receive full immediate
enumeration if the budget permits; larger hands get a bounded legal pool and
an explicit incomplete-search warning.

If a play clears the blind, conservation/tie-breaks can prefer fewer cards and
avoid unnecessary consumption. Against The Arm, lost hand-level value is
estimated from hand level/use, deck composition and Joker support; a Level 1
Three of a Kind can be chosen over a valuable upgraded Pair if both clear.

For non-clearing plays, cycling considers useful non-scoring extras rather than
always playing a lone high card. It preserves valuable held cards, respects
effects such as Half Joker and single-card DNA/Sixth Sense, and avoids careless
padding against The Tooth or Double or Nothing. This is a bounded extra-card
comparison, not an exhaustive search over all immediate-score sacrifices.

Existing early stopping is incomplete: `search.run` returns before discard
sampling when its selected play clears, but this follows immediate subset
enumeration, and `decision.run` still invokes later specialist passes. This is
not the user's newly requested first-clearing-candidate stop across the whole
decision. The reported approximately 130,000 comparisons have not been traced
to a particular pass; instrument the complete decision rather than assuming
discard sampling caused them. The required fast path is specified in §9.

Promising discards get matched replacement samples from the remaining deck.
Using the same sample streams makes candidates more comparable. The displayed
discard percentage is the fraction of sampled draws whose **estimated next
play** clears; it is not a calibrated run-win probability. On a final hand,
clear probability takes priority over losing average chips.

Supported ordinary builds can compare play-now versus selected discard lines
over at most four remaining hands. Later plays are greedy and later discards
are not fully searched. Future Glass/Hook/DNA and several discard-trigger
effects block portions of this comparison. When support is missing, the system
falls back instead of presenting invented survival evidence.

### 4.4 Separate action passes and their limitations

`decision.run` currently runs these hand passes in order:

1. Play/discard/cycling/remaining-blind search.
2. Held consumables, including a small class of two-consumable rescues.
3. Joker ordering, comparing a common subset set under alternative orders.
4. Played-card ordering, permuting only the already chosen card set.
5. A boss-disable sale that produces a verified immediate modeled clear.

Accepted final-action priority is boss rescue, played-card reorder, Joker reorder,
consumable, discard, then play. Each specialist has guards that decide whether
its proposal is acceptable; this is not a blind override of all earlier results.
For example, boss sales preserve existing clearing plans, and played-card
ordering now preserves an existing deterministic Joker-order clear that might
save Glass or permanently debuffed cards.

This architecture is **not a unified optimizer over complete action sequences**.
It can miss “different played subset + different order + Tarot” or “discard,
then use a Tarot on the resulting cards.” Better individual modules do not
automatically solve those joint decisions. Do not describe current separate
passes as full multi-action search.

### 4.5 Consumables and ordering

The pure consumable simulator covers all Planets, Black Hole, eight enhancement
Tarots, four suit conversions, Strength, Death, Hermit and Temperance (29 keys).
It accounts for targets/direction, hand levels, relevant counters and removal
from inventory. A small rescue search can evaluate up to eight two-item pairs,
typically a Planet/Black Hole followed by a targeted Tarot. It requires a
deterministic modeled joint clear and executes only the first item, then
recomputes. It is not an arbitrary two-consumable sequence optimizer.

Joker ordering considers pins, compatibility, copy chains/loops and editions,
and a common hand-subset set. It is bounded for large rows. Played-card ordering
evaluates at most 120 permutations of up to five chosen cards, returning a full
hand permutation that leaves unselected slots fixed. This preserves held Steel,
Raised Fist and other unplayed-card ordering effects. Execute reorders only;
the next fresh recommendation decides the actual play.

### 4.6 Shop, pack and economy decisions

`strategy.lua` contains handwritten ratings and thresholds for tactical strength,
income, scaling, hand affinity, deck composition, purchase cost, interest,
inventory limits, challenge rules and known synergies. These strategic numbers
are **not learned or calibrated**. Pack targeting is mostly utility-based and
does not simulate a whole deck-development trajectory.

`shop_scoring` adds four fixed paired deck-draw comparisons for supported
before/after purchase or sale states. It updates resources/cash and derived
Joker caches such as Swashbuckler and Stencil. Copy-Joker comparisons allow up
to eight sensible layouts on each side, so a purchase does not get credit
merely for fixing an order that was already available for free.

Supported Card Sharp/Acrobat/Dusk/Mystic Summit changes can receive a discounted
late-hand scenario. Its repeat target is fixed before comparing purchases; this
is conditional capacity, not a probability that the condition will activate.
Unknown discard paths or blind-start mutations still fall back. The baseline
uses a neutral blind; current shop scores do not fully forecast the actual next
boss. If the shared shop comparison budget cannot finish a whole decision,
all tactical adjustments are discarded in favor of strategic ratings.

Synergy forecasts distinguish owned partners, visible combinations and future
opportunities. The current catalog includes examples such as Photograph/Chad,
Wee/Chad/Hack, Baron/Mime, DNA/Hologram and Fibonacci/Hack. Speculative odds use
eligible rarity pools, actual shop rates/slots, known exclusions, reroll costs,
reserved money and at most three future shops. They assume survival, approximate
independent draws and omit many future events. They are neither exhaustive
synergy discovery nor run-win predictions.

Omelette Egg sales require a useful visible opportunity. Temperance may be used
before spending/selling; resale growth is not confused with spendable income.
Paid rerolls reserve purchase cash, rent and near-term costs, protect interest
when appropriate, do not borrow against hypothetical income, and avoid treating
mature scaling Jokers or an Egg feeding Swashbuckler as disposable filler.

### 4.7 Boss rescues, publication and execution

Boss rescue can sell an active Luchador, or a supported sellable Joker against
Verdant Leaf, if the post-sale model yields an immediate deterministic clear.
It models target reductions (Wall /2, Violet Vessel /3), Water/Needle resources,
permanent-debuff preservation, Campfire timing, derived row caches and capacity.
Unknown added draws/sorts or special sale effects are deliberately withheld.
This is not a general economic decision to sell whenever a boss is inconvenient.

`runtime` publishes only a finished decision. HUD, panel and execution use the
same result/fingerprint. `Execute` rechecks live state and legality, latches the
decision before invoking a callback, and refuses duplicate clicks. A known
preflight rejection can release the latch; a callback that may have started
keeps it until the state changes. Reordering, selling and consumable actions
require fresh advice afterward. The UI never queues an unattended action loop.

## 5. Search budgets and responsiveness already implemented

| Component | Current limit or behavior |
|---|---|
| Play/discard/cycling/continuation | Shared 140,000 score evaluations |
| Immediate hand enumeration | Up to 20 held cards; budgeted legal fallback above that |
| Cycling / remaining-blind state size | At most 12 held cards |
| Discards | Up to 16 candidate sets and normally up to 24 matched samples, reduced to fit budget |
| Cycling | Up to 13 extra-card candidates and up to 12 paired draws |
| Remaining-blind continuation | At most 4 hands, normally up to 8 samples; at least 4 complete paired samples |
| Inventory consumables | Shared 25,000 evaluations, including bounded two-item rescue |
| Joker ordering | 30,000 evaluations; up to 720 orders, bounded subset shortlist where needed |
| Played-card ordering | At most 120 permutations; options also have a hard 10,000 ceiling |
| Boss rescue | 10,000 evaluations; complete comparisons only |
| Effective maximum hand-decision score count | 205,120 across the above passes, not a latency guarantee |
| Shop comparisons | Shared 50,000 evaluations; 4 paired draws, up to 8 layouts, 1 or 2 temporal scenarios |
| Idle state refresh | Approximately every 0.3 seconds |
| Working-state refresh | Every active worker slice/frame to prevent stale publication |
| Cooperative work slice | About 4 ms of coroutine resumes per frame; a single batch can overrun |
| Typical yield cadence | Most passes every 32 scores; shop often every 64 |

Crucial performance caveat: snapshot copying and canonical fingerprinting occur
before/outside the coroutine slice, and a batch is only interruptible at its
yield. The four-millisecond setting is **not a hard total-frame cap**. This is
an obvious area to measure before changing algorithms or adding more lookahead.

## 6. Limited challenge opening search and installed progress

### Separate opening route

Settings path: **Brainstorm → Challenge opening**. Choose zero to two distinct
Legendary targets, using Any for unspecified slots. Start explicitly with the
button or the configured auto-reroll hotkey. It works only in the settled first
Small Blind selection of a fresh challenge: Ante 1, round 0, no skips, original
supported starting inventory/slots and compatible bans. Progressed runs cannot
be replaced by this feature.

Native interface:

```c
const char* brainstorm_challenge_opening_v1(
    const char* seed, const char* challenge_id, const char* targetJokersCSV);
void free_result(const char* result);
```

The endpoint accepts zero to two different Legendary names or `j_` keys, seeks
first-Small Charm and at least two natural Soul rolls, and returns structured
JSON with found/not_found/error status, matching challenge, Soul count,
Legendary order, required sales and continuation seed. Each batch is capped at
one million candidates, honors a smaller `BRAINSTORM_SEARCH_LIMIT`, and uses
the existing balanced/maximum native CPU setting. Native seed enumeration can
return short seeds; a continuation cursor can be empty at wrap. The Lua parser
validates this narrow transport and never executes result text.

The original first-Charm duplicate-Soul exception clears only the Soul duplicate
guard during creation of that one matching pack. It does not grant Jokers,
change other Tarot bans or increase the pack's two choices. Vanilla otherwise
suppresses the second Soul. Challenge identity/round/skip flags and a claimed
pack marker keep the exception scoped. Error cleanup restores the duplicate
guard even if card creation fails.

The live challenge definition object is retained when the user starts a matched
run. Existing filtered-run handling records `used_filter`/`filter_info` and sets
`seeded=false`; this is inherited reroller behavior, not a direct achievement
grant. The game's achievement handling remains separate.

Supported: all nineteen vanilla challenges with usable Joker slots. Jokerless
bans Soul and is excluded. Omelette needs two legal Egg sales for both Souls;
the advisor recommends one sale only when a visible Soul needs space. Blast Off
has exactly two free starting slots. The native allowlist is not general support
for arbitrary modded challenges.

Current source proof: seed `I1L21111` produces first Small Charm, two Souls,
then Yorick and Perkeo on all nineteen supported challenge starts. Original
source initialization, card generation, Egg sales and Soul uses agree. The
vanilla no-exception control produces one Soul; Jokerless remains banned.
One separate balanced-CPU Any/Any native call returned in 1.85 ms, stopping
early; this is not a throughput or selected-pair ETA guarantee.

Two Legendaries alone do not imply a strong opening. Triboulet needs scoring
Kings/Queens, Yorick needs discards, Canio needs destruction and Perkeo needs
useful consumables. Automatic pair suitability/progression planning is still
an improvement opportunity, not a property of the current Any/Any default.

### What the latest batches accomplished

- Earlier work introduced detached scoring, play/discard advice, challenge/boss
  context, consumable handling, synergy forecasts and Joker ordering.
- The popup inconsistency was addressed through stable snapshots and final-only
  publication; the high-card issue was addressed through useful non-scoring
  card cycling rather than blindly selecting one card.
- v2.14 added the adjacent one-click Execute workflow with live preflight and
  duplicate-action protection.
- v2.15 added scoring-backed shop decisions, broader remaining-blind comparison
  and bounded two-consumable rescues.
- v2.16 added played-card ordering, fair copy-Joker shop comparisons, conditional
  later-hand shop capacity, boss-rescue sales, budgeted paid rerolls, and the
  challenge opening route. Hotkey debouncing and search-failure cleanup avoid
  repeated alerts/stale progress displays.

Last validation: **28/28 Lua fixtures, 15/15 Python protocol tests**, native
opening/normal-API/Soul regressions, and 21 short original-source opening cases.
Useful regression evidence includes Photograph/Chad ordering 1,700→5,600 chips
and a Glass/Mult Straight 600→800. These demonstrate mechanics improvements,
not population win rates.

Development episode evidence is deliberately modest. An early `ADVISOR1`
Omelette pilot lost at Ante 2; nine deterministic scores matched source. A
v2.15 `ADVISOR5` probe resolved 30 actions and stopped intentionally in an Ante 2
shop. A v2.16 `ADVISOR6` probe resolved 20 actions in 19.87 seconds and stopped
after opening a Celestial pack after Big Blind; its four deterministic scores
were 16, 280, 15 and 724, matching source. These are development seeds and
synthetic unlock profiles, with `qualification_compatible=false`, not held-out
full-run success measurements. Final source edits can differ from the digest
loaded by a retained probe.

Do not re-read megabyte logs by default. Begin with `engine_probe_report.md`;
open a trace only for a specific counterexample. Logs are under
`tools/advisor_eval`, including `engine_probe_v215_ADVISOR5.log` and
`engine_probe_v216_ADVISOR6.log`.

## 7. Joker progression: current coverage and proposed design

### Three different levels of support

1. **Read accumulated strength:** mostly implemented. Full ability tables carry
   current Mult/chips/XMult, thresholds and counters into the snapshot.
2. **Predict the next action's changes:** partly implemented. Several scoring
   and play triggers work; many discard, destruction, blind-start, generation
   and end-round transitions do not.
3. **Invest deliberately for later rounds:** mostly heuristic or absent. Some
   supported growth influences same-blind lookahead, but there is no complete
   progression planner estimating the value of future trigger opportunities.

Do not tell the user that all progression is missing, or that reading a mature
Joker's current XMult means its future progression is solved.

### Verified existing progression examples

| Effect | Existing behavior | Location / limitation |
|---|---|---|
| Trousers, Square, Runner, Green | Before-scoring growth | `scoring.score`, around line 224; physical-Joker growth is not multiplied by Blueprint |
| Ride the Bus | Scoring-face reset or growth | `scoring.lua` ~231 |
| Obelisk | Current hand-count reset/growth rule | ~234; establishing a long-run lead and pivot remains unsolved |
| Midas / Vampire | Ordered enhancement changes and Vampire growth | ~237 |
| Wee / Hiker | Growth and permanent chips from supported scoring retriggers | ~293 |
| Lucky Cat / Space Joker | Expected-value approximations | ~248/286/298; not exact stochastic outcome transitions |
| Persistent supported effects | Carried to detached next-play state | `after_play` ~421 |
| Ice Cream / Seltzer | Per-play decline and expiry/removal | ~501; updates relevant capacity/tallies |
| Green discard / Ramen | Once-per-action Green loss; per-card Ramen loss and possible removal | `search.discard_score_penalties` ~73 |
| Constellation / usage-based value | Planet use grows each physical Constellation once, tracks usage | `consumables.apply` ~157/209 |
| Campfire | Growth in supported sale projections | `strategy.lua`; `boss_rescue.lua` respects disable/sale timing |
| Growth-aware Joker order | Limited growth/economy/destruction tie-breaks | `ordering.future_metrics` ~148 |

Relevant evidence: `tests/advisor_play_state.lua`, `advisor_resource_decisions.lua`,
`advisor_consumables.lua`, `advisor_ordering.lua`, `advisor_boss_rescue.lua` and
`advisor_strategy.lua`. These exercise specific mechanisms, not whole-run growth
strategies.

### Yorick is a concrete first patch

The scorer reads Yorick's live `ability.x_mult`, but `search.lua` explicitly
blocks remaining-blind continuation with Yorick. Ordinary discard sampling still
runs, but its limited discard transition does not add Yorick's progress.

There is also a valuation disconnect: `strategy.lua`'s generic `scaling` and
`xmult` maps omit Yorick, and `joker_value` has no Yorick-specific growth case.
A fresh Yorick can receive the default strategic rating (22), while a mature
one's current multiplier is scored correctly. The opening search can therefore
obtain a Joker whose development the advisor does not plan well.

Authoritative installed-source logic, historically extracted in
`C:/Users/trevo/AppData/Local/Temp/brainstorm-advisor-source/card.lua` around
2788–2799:

- Process **each discarded card**, not just each discard action.
- If `ability.yorick_discards <= 1`, reset it to `ability.extra.discards` and
  increase `ability.x_mult` by `ability.extra.xmult`.
- Otherwise decrement `yorick_discards`.
- `not context.blueprint`: a Blueprint does not multiply Yorick's own progress.
- Installed defaults in `game.lua` ~524 are 23 discarded cards per +1 XMult.
  Use live snapshotted fields, not hardcoded defaults.

Neighboring original-source trigger locations are useful: Burnt ~2749, Trading
Card ~2802, Castle ~2814, Mail ~2825, Hit the Road ~2835, Green ~2846, Faceless
~2858; Hit the Road resets at end round near ~3011. If the temporary source is
missing or the game updates, read the installed archive and verify again.

### Proposed shared transition layer — not yet created

Create a pure `after_discard(snapshot, discarded_indices, context)` transition,
then share it between one-discard evaluation and remaining-blind continuation.
Possible future file: `Brainstorm/Advisor/transitions.lua`; **it does not exist**
at handoff. Avoid duplicating another partial implementation in each planner.

First migrate Green/Ramen without changing results, then add Yorick and Castle.
Next add Burnt's first-discard upgrade, Mail/Faceless payouts and Hit the Road,
with exact callback eligibility. Trading Card requires actual population
removal/cash; Purple seal generation needs capacity, eligible pools and sampled
unknown outputs. Remove each continuation blocker only after its transition is
represented and validated in every path that uses it.

Later unify `after_play`, consumable use, sales, rerolls, blind start and end
round behind consistent detached event semantics. The scoring source oracle
must establish trigger order, copied versus physical growth, debuffs, removals,
Negative slots, cash, and derived state. A common layer should reduce drift
between shop previews, blind tactics and simulations; this is a proposal, not a
reason to rewrite all working modules before a small Yorick fix.

Acceptance examples for the first slice:

- At the threshold, discarding one card grows Yorick and resets the counter.
- A multi-card discard advances once per discarded card and can cross thresholds
  correctly with custom live counters.
- A debuffed discarded playing card still advances an active Yorick; a debuffed
  Yorick does not trigger. Unlike Castle and Hit the Road, Yorick's source branch
  has no discarded-card debuff guard.
- Blueprint does not create extra permanent progress; scoring copies still use
  the resulting strength according to actual rules.
- Debuffed/disabled effects and ordering match source.
- Both ordinary discard evaluation and continuation use the same transition.
- Inputs remain unchanged; no global RNG or live callbacks are called.
- A deliberately constructed losing/clearing choice changes because growth is
  accounted for, and source execution confirms the predicted result.
- A current safe clear is not delayed merely to farm growth that cannot repay
  its time/resource/risk cost.

### Future growth value: the part to calibrate

For supported exact transitions, the increment itself is a fact. The uncertain
part is how much that increment will help over the remaining run. Useful
features include current counter distance to the next increment, attainable
triggers from actual deck composition, remaining hands/discards/money, expected
future rounds, expiration/reset conditions, current scoring bottleneck, hand
levels, synergy multipliers, upcoming boss, and cost of taking extra actions.

Compare **marginal future score or survival value**, not raw +chips versus raw
+Mult units. The same +10 chips can matter differently depending on current
Mult, XMult and the blind requirement. Growth should be discounted when it is
unlikely to trigger in time or when the Joker may be sold/destroyed/expire.
Discount unnecessary investment if the remaining run already has little to
gain, especially at its final win. Do not equate an easy current blind with
that condition: being safely ahead early can make investment more attractive
because it is affordable and has many future blinds to pay back. A near-threshold
Yorick can justify a discard even if the redraw itself offers little immediate value.

One candidate heuristic is:

`action value = immediate survival/progress + weighted future growth value
                + economy/deck value - resource cost - added completion time`

The weights/forecast horizon may be tuned. The underlying thresholds, legality,
physical triggers, score arithmetic and actual cash changes must not be tuned.
These features and coefficients have not been centralized or calibrated yet.

### Burnt Joker: exact first-discard levels and dynamic strategic choice

**Requested, not implemented.** This is the Joker the user describes as
increasing the level of the first discarded hand. Owning or buying it should
lead to a build-aware plan for that recurring upgrade, not a fixed instruction
to discard whichever hand currently scores highest.

Verified original-source rules, from the installed archive in §3:

- `card.lua` ~2748–2754 runs Burnt on `context.pre_discard` when
  `current_round.discards_used <= 0` and `not context.hook`, subject to the
  active/non-debuffed Joker guard around ~2291. Use those exact conditions.
- It classifies `G.hand.highlighted` and upgrades the returned actual poker
  category once per active effect. A selected Full House upgrades Full House,
  not the Pair it contains; a Royal Flush uses the Straight Flush category.
  See `functions/state_events.lua` ~540–568 for the classifier.
- Pre-discard runs before individual discarded-card effects/removal
  (`functions/state_events.lua` ~394–404). Correct level/resource state must
  carry into subsequent draw and play evaluation.
- Valid Blueprint/Brainstorm copies add upgrades: Burnt has no
  `not context.blueprint` restriction. Do not reuse Yorick's physical-growth
  exclusion for this effect. Respect actual copy chains/order and debuffs.

Current limits: `search.continuation_discard_blocker` explicitly blocks Burnt;
the discard projection does not apply its level upgrade. `scoring.lua` treats
Burnt as passive during the played hand, and strategy has no dynamic first-
discard investment planner. Implement the shared exact discard transition and
source-parity cases before removing that blocker or claiming reliable lookahead.

Choose among **feasible first-discard selections** by marginal remaining-run
benefit, using the shared build profile and investment allowance:

- Deck composition and attainable hand frequency, current levels and each
  category's chip/Mult gain per level, hand history, conditional/copy/scaling
  Jokers, Observatory/Planet stock, deck-development targets and future bosses.
- The cost of losing those particular held cards, replacement probabilities,
  remaining discards/hands, challenge economy and the ability to finish this
  blind after the upgrade. Compare a valuable difficult hand against a smaller
  hand that is cheaper to discard and likely to score repeatedly later.
- Immediate versus future horizon, a possible pivot, and diminishing returns.
  A high-level main hand may still deserve the upgrade; a lower-level backup
  may sometimes be better. Level alone, hand size alone and usage count alone
  are not valid universal rankings. Level changes are not played-hand usage.
- Interactions with Yorick and other discard effects; combine their exact
  changes with the level upgrade rather than selecting separately contradictory
  plans. Re-evaluate when the first-discard opportunity is spent or the state
  changes. Shop value for Burnt should reflect realistically usable future
  upgrades and opportunity cost, not an unconditional acquisition preference.

Keep the choice bounded: shortlist useful build categories and legal selected
subsets, apply exact transitions, and use cheap future-value estimates before
costlier continuation. Preserve the fast path's small investment allowance.
Explain the choice, for example “First-discard Pair for a level; this deck can
use Pairs consistently,” only when that is actually supported by the state.

Acceptance cases: deck/Joker/level changes switch the preferred hand, including
a winner that is neither most-used nor highest-level; selected Full House
upgrades the correct category; second/Hook/debuffed cases do not invent an
upgrade; valid copied effects add levels; and a comfortable investment is
rejected in a comparable fragile state that cannot safely discard those cards.
Add these to the shared discard tests, `tests/advisor_resource_decisions.lua`,
`tests/advisor_strategy.lua` and source-parity probes when implementing.

### Latest clarification: use a comfortable position to invest in future strength

**Requested general policy, not implemented.** Local score, spare-hand payout
and minimum actions are not independent objectives to maximize at all times.
The user explicitly wants to trade some of those resources for development
that improves later survival or reduces expected time to finish the challenge.
The willingness to invest should increase with verified current safety and a
useful remaining horizon, all else equal. This extends the earlier cheap
upgrade exception to bounded play/discard sequences before taking a clear.

Two concrete examples must become regression cases:

- **Death target search:** spend spare played hands to replace expendable cards
  and look for a valuable source card to copy, even when those plays reduce
  the blind's eventual income. Value the resulting copy/deck concentration,
  not merely the low score of the cycling hand. The copying plan needs both a
  worthwhile source and a suitable recipient, legal targets/order and an
  available Death after any other planned uses. It may also need to preserve
  a valuable last Perkeo copying source as specified below.
- **Yorick investment:** prefer a larger discard when its additional discarded
  cards produce valuable progress toward current/future XMult thresholds and
  the retained/drawn cards still support completion. Progress counts per card,
  not per discard action; include residual progress toward later thresholds,
  rather than valuing only an immediate threshold crossing. More discards is
  a conditional preference, not an unconditional instruction to discard five.
  Played cards can cycle the hand but do not count as Yorick discards.

Use an inexpensive **investment allowance** derived from the actual state:
reliable scoring capacity relative to remaining chips, spare hands/discards,
retained finishing cards, deck draw opportunities, current cash/reserves,
Joker/boss/challenge effects, and remaining blinds. Include uncertainty and
fragility of the planned clear; a large expected score from random effects is
not equivalent to a dependable reserve. Being overpowered in this blind does
not establish that future bosses or scaling requirements are solved.

Compare the whole bounded plan against finishing now:

`investment value = expected future development benefit
                    - lost income/interest and consumable opportunity cost
                    - added current failure risk and irreversible damage
                    - added action/recommendation time`

The strategic conversion weights are heuristic until calibrated. Immediate
resource changes and trigger rules must stay exact. Use the actual challenge
economy instead of a universal cash penalty per extra hand; include effects
such as discard costs, lost held-card value, Glass loss and permanent debuffs.
Allow lower current income when the investment's expected future return is
larger. Conversely, current comfort alone cannot justify negative-value
farming, and a longer horizon is not proof that every upgrade is useful.

For Death search, use remaining-deck composition without peeking at its hidden
order. If `M` eligible targets are among `N` remaining cards and a plan really
draws `d` distinct cards without replacement, the simple chance of seeing at
least one is `1 - choose(N-M,d)/choose(N,d)`. Use this only where its assumptions
hold; special draw effects, target/recipient retention and later actions can
change the event being valued. If no eligible target remains drawable, do not
burn hands looking for it. Distinguish a probabilistic future target from the
known finishing resources that remain if the search fails.

Every investment must have a bounded stopping rule and a viable way to finish.
For play-based cycling, the setup play must **not already clear the blind**:
once it clears, the planned within-blind draw/Death use is no longer available.
Keep enough hands/cards/scoring capacity to finish after a failed search, and
recompute after every real draw. Stop when the target is found and used, the
benefit shrinks below cost, the allowance is exhausted or the safety margin
falls. Do not repeatedly restart the same budget to farm indefinitely. Preserve
normal one-action-per-click execution and stable completed advice.

Reconcile this with §9: finding a clear stops broad tactical search, but one
small strategic check may choose a specific positive-value setup play/discard
instead of immediately finishing. Use a few relevant candidate plans, cached
features, cheap draw odds and exact supported transitions; do not rerun
130,000 comparisons simply because investment is now allowed. Explain the
intent briefly, e.g. “Cycle these cards to find a King for Death; keep a hand
to finish” or “Discard four for Yorick growth; the retained hand still clears.”
Such statements must describe the modeled resources, not invent guarantees.

Required tests when this work is implemented:

- A strong early state chooses a useful Death-target cycling play or larger
  Yorick discard despite lower immediate cash/score; the same opportunity in
  a fragile state preserves completion instead.
- A target-present state uses the appropriate copy plan; a target-absent deck
  and a plan whose setup play already wins the blind do not claim future draws.
- Yorick growth, selected cards, remaining counters and the post-investment
  finishing line agree with original-source mechanics, including paid-discard
  and relevant boss/challenge costs.
- No worthwhile remaining horizon, exhausted allowance or a final challenge
  win stops development. Costs and Perkeo retention can reverse a superficially
  attractive plan; growth is not automatically worth sacrificing the engine.
- The short investment check has a hard computation cap and cannot re-enable
  all tactical specialists or publish a provisional action that later changes.

Implementation touchpoints: `search.run`/`choose_cycle`, the proposed shared
discard transition, `consumables.apply`/target selection, `decision.run`,
strategy build/growth features and economy accounting. Existing cycling aims
at supported tactical continuation, and later rollout plays are greedy; those
features do not yet implement intentional multi-step Death-target development.
Add cases to `tests/advisor_cycling.lua`, `tests/advisor_resource_decisions.lua`,
`tests/advisor_consumables.lua` and `tests/advisor_decision_integration.lua` as
the shared implementation lands. Do not claim a calibrated gain from these
proposed examples alone.

### Latest user counterexample: preserve Perkeo's useful copying source

**Requested, not implemented or reproduced in a fixture.** The user reports a
run with two Perkeos and a Strength Tarot supporting repeated rank upgrades
toward Four/Five of a Kind. The advisor recommended consuming the last Strength,
which removes the source needed to generate further Strength copies. The user's
belief that this engine could reach Ante 8 is strategic feedback, not a measured
success rate. Preserve the full counterexample in future regression work.

The planner must value a useful retained consumable as a source of future cards,
not only its immediate effect. This differs from the general value assigned to
owning Perkeo in `strategy.joker_value`; the cost must enter the actual use/sell
and action-sequence decisions. Relevant files are `consumables.lua`,
`strategy.lua`, `economy.lua`, `decision.lua` and the shared detached transition
work, all under the Advisor directory in §3.

The read-only source audit confirms Perkeo triggers at **shop exit**
(`card.lua` ~2412–2425; `functions/button_callbacks.lua` ~2481–2486 inside the
original game archive). Each active effect randomly copies an inventory
consumable and makes the copy Negative. Negative consumables are themselves
eligible sources: retain at least one useful Strength, not necessarily the
original non-Negative card. Two active Perkeos with only Strengths available
generate two further Strengths. Mixed inventory changes the target odds, and
later queued copy events can see earlier additions. Compatible Blueprint/
Brainstorm effects depend on actual Joker order and copy chains. A blind skip
or played hand is not itself a shop-exit trigger.

The current `consumables.suggest` already suppresses targeted Tarot use when
the baseline clears and usually when an immediate discard forecast clears at
least 98% of its samples. Those guards are not Perkeo retention: otherwise the
use ranking focuses on immediate rescue/capped score. `economy.suggest` can
also spend a final cash Tarot without valuing the future copying source.
The existing suppression of targeted Tarot use on a clearing baseline is a
current limitation, not a requirement to preserve unchanged: it must allow the
cheap, strategically useful development case below without reopening a broad
tactical search.

Proposed behavior and acceptance criteria:

- Recognize the last **useful copy of a particular consumable**, especially
  Strength in a rank-consolidation build, even if other consumables remain.
  Keeping an unrelated item does not preserve the Strength engine.
- Prefer using surplus copies while retaining at least one useful source when
  future copying opportunities make it valuable. Track actual inventory after
  each action, including two-item sequences; a sequence must not accidentally
  consume the last source after its first step appeared safe.
- Account for active Perkeos, compatible copying Jokers, debuffs, eligible
  inventory, copy-target randomness, future trigger opportunities and remaining
  useful rank upgrades. Do not assume two Perkeos always produce two Strengths
  when their actual target pool contains other cards. Use original-source
  trigger timing, rather than assuming generation after every played hand.
- Keep exact copying rules separate from the heuristic future value. With no
  active generator or no future useful generation opportunity, the retention
  premium should disappear. Rank development must consider Strength's actual
  rank changes and current deck composition; more uses are not always better.
- Do not spend the last useful source just for overkill, a small immediate
  score increase, or a replaceable benefit. A hand that already clears without
  spending it should use the fast path in §9.
- Allow an explicit survival exception when consuming it is needed to avoid
  losing the run, and allow a final challenge-winning use when there is no
  remaining generation horizon. This is a strong conditional preference, not
  an unconditional ban that sacrifices a win to preserve inventory.
- Cover one Strength/two Perkeos, surplus Strengths, mixed consumable types,
  a sole Negative Strength, generator debuffs/removal, multi-item sequences and
  immediate/final-win exceptions. Extend the same source logic to useful Death,
  Planet, Hermit and Temperance engines. Include a brief rationale such as
  “Keep the last Strength for Perkeo copies” in completed advice when retention
  changes the choice.

This source-retention rule belongs ahead of broad calibration: tuning immediate
consumable weights cannot reliably compensate for omitting the generation
engine entirely. Long-term value can be tuned once the source and triggers are
represented correctly.

### Latest Perkeo requirement: copying-pool composition and Observatory

**Requested generalization, not implemented.** Keeping one useful source is
necessary for many engines, but insufficient. Buying or retaining another
consumable changes what Perkeo can copy. The user's example is sole Strength
followed by a suggested Hierophant purchase despite preferring Strength copies.
Hierophant is not universally worse: compare the marginal value of the actual
build, inventory, remaining opportunities and action sequence. Apply the same
reasoning to all eligible Tarots, Planets and Spectrals.

Treat each actual inventory card as a copy target, including Negative copies.
For an otherwise ordinary uniform pool with `N` cards and `n_t` copies of type
`t`, the next copy has probability `n_t / N` of being that type. Thus:

| Inventory at the next copy event | Strength chance | Hierophant chance |
|---|---:|---:|
| One Strength | 100% | 0% |
| One Strength + one Hierophant | 50% | 50% |
| Nine Strengths, including Negative copies, + one Hierophant | 90% | 10% |

The growing preferred stock makes holding a useful secondary card less costly
in copying opportunity, all else equal. Count **cards**, not unique types or
occupied normal slots. Conversely, consuming surplus preferred cards changes
the proportions again, even when one source remains. Recompute after buying,
using, selling and actual generation; source-retention logic alone misses this.

For multiple Perkeo/copy-Joker effects, project the actual sequential events.
Each generated Negative card joins the pool before a later copy event can
select it. Do not model those events as independent draws from a frozen pool,
or round expected counts into a fictitious deterministic inventory. Starting
with one Strength and one Hierophant, two copies yield both Strengths with
probability `(1/2)*(2/3) = 1/3`, not 1/4. Under this simple uniform reinforcement
process, expected added Strengths in `k` events remain `k*n_strength/N`, but
outcome probabilities are correlated. Intervening use/sale, eligibility changes
and future shop exits require their own state transitions.

Use one shared **marginal inventory value** when comparing buy/skip, hold/use,
sell/keep, pack picks and feasible short sequences. Value the resulting state,
including direct utility, held effects, cash/interest/slots, future copy value,
foregone copies of alternatives and time. A cheap finite-event model can use:

`F_0(I) = strategic value of inventory I`

`F_k(I) = sum over eligible types t of P(t | I) * F_(k-1)(I + Negative copy(t))`

Compare complete alternative plans against the same baseline, adding only
their distinct immediate effects/costs. Avoid counting the same generation
benefit both in a Joker rating and again in each consumable rating. Keep exact
selection/event rules fixed; marginal strategic values and future opportunity
discounts are the parts to calibrate. Group equivalent cards and memoize only
a bounded number of events, using cached build features rather than a large
new hand-scoring search. No such shared evaluator exists yet.

Evaluate **inventory at the actual shop exit**. Buying and legally using or
selling a temporary card before that exit may avoid dilution, but do not
assume targeted Tarots can be used in a phase with no legal targets. A proposed
sequence must account for price, use/sale value, available targets and execution
order. If the extra card remains at exit, include both its dilution cost and
the possibility that its copies grow and further dilute the preferred type.
Do not promise that a presently small unwanted fraction stays small on every
future trajectory.

**Observatory integration:** the voucher described by the user is Observatory
(`v_observatory`), which follows Telescope. The source archive's `game.lua`
~614 sets its vanilla factor to 1.5; `card.lua` ~2291–2299 applies it for each
non-debuffed held Planet matching the scored hand. Thus `m` such cards give
`1.5^m` from this effect, including eligible Negative copies. The current
`scoring.lua` ~395–400 already applies that immediate effect. Owned Planet use
in `consumables.apply` removes the card and raises the hand level, so its
immediate rescore can reflect losing that held multiplier. The missing work is
the general long-term inventory/purchase/copy valuation; Observatory should
not be described as entirely absent from the scorer.

Compare holding a matching Planet for its multiplier and future copies against
using it for a permanent level, and against keeping a rank-development Tarot
or a different Planet engine. Use actual hand levels, deck consistency, Joker
conditions and how often each hand is expected to score. Unmatched Planets do
not grant that hand's Observatory multiplier, but can still have other future
value. Do not indiscriminately hoard every Planet or always spend them.
With a nonlinear held multiplier, `E[1.5^K]` is not `1.5^E[K]`; use the small
outcome distribution or label a bounded approximation, rather than reporting
fractional average inventory as an exact score/clear guarantee.

Acceptance cases for this generalization:

- A weaker long-term addition to sole Strength is rejected when its direct
  benefit cannot repay lost useful copying; swap the build's values and allow
  that same named card when it is the better plan.
- One preferred card versus nine preferred cards plus the same extra card
  produces the correct 50% versus 90% next-copy odds; the marginal dilution
  penalty decreases, and an otherwise useful purchase can become worthwhile.
- Two sequential copy events use updated inventory; Negative editions count
  as sources, and using/selling preferred copies updates later odds correctly.
- With Observatory and a matching Planet engine, compare buy/hold/copy/use
  through both level and held-multiplier effects. Cover unmatched Planets,
  no Observatory, card debuffs and different favored-hand levels/builds.
- Feasible buy-and-use-before-exit plans differ from buy-and-hold plans; an
  illegal assumed use must never hide dilution.
- No active generator or no remaining generation opportunities removes the
  copying premium, while current Observatory held value can remain relevant.
- Runtime advice, pack/shop valuations and economy actions use the same
  inventory values and give a concise reason when dilution changes a choice.

Implementation touchpoints: `strategy.consumable_value`, purchase/pack and
voucher valuation, `economy.suggest`, `consumables.suggest`/`apply`, snapshot
inventory/edition/voucher state, and the shared generation transition. Relevant
fixtures are `tests/advisor_strategy.lua`, `tests/advisor_shop_decisions.lua`,
`tests/advisor_economy.lua`, `tests/advisor_consumables.lua` and
`tests/advisor_scoring.lua`. The exact local source paths are in §3. These are
future regression requirements, not tests added or run in this documentation update.

### Latest user counterexample: invest upgrades in the established rank build

**Requested, not implemented or reproduced.** The user reports a Kings-heavy
deck pursuing Five of a Kind, yet advice to upgrade an Ace. The exact inventory,
upgrade and snapshot have not been captured, so do not claim a particular
numeric tie-break caused that live recommendation. The general failure is
short-sighted targeting: an incidental card's current contribution can win out
over investment in the cards intended to score repeatedly over the run.

The read-only audit identifies two concrete gaps. `consumables.suggest` ranks
owned-card targets by immediate best-play score, blind-clear benefit, Arm cost
and target count, with no separate future rank-concentration objective.
`strategy.lua` has `favored_hand`, `deck_stats` and `target_value` affinity
features, but `choose_pack_targets` compares after-minus-before value: unchanged
rank-affinity terms can cancel, and equal improvements fall back to card index.
This is consistent with the report; it does not establish which path selected
that particular Ace. Future usage should weight the **marginal upgrade itself**,
rather than be a constant added to both sides that disappears in subtraction.

Add a shared, inexpensive build profile using actual deck rank/suit counts,
hand levels and usage, Joker conditions, enhancements, available transformations
and remaining opportunities. It should recognize a strong existing commitment
without inventing one from a single hand. For a deck dominated by Kings with
Five of a Kind support, an enhancement on a King normally has more future use
than the same enhancement on a rarely played Ace. Existing hand/deck affinity
in shop strategy does not by itself establish that inventory targeting applies
the same long-term preference.

Requirements and focused regressions:

- Prefer enhancing cards central to the established scoring/held-card engine,
  using expected future participation, retriggers and relevant Joker effects.
  Raw rank/chips or immediate target score alone cannot determine investment.
- Compare the **result of each transformation** to the deck plan. Strength on
  a Queen makes another King; Strength on a King makes an Ace and may undermine
  the build. Death can turn an off-plan card into a useful King when the copy
  direction is correct. Do not implement a blanket “never target non-Kings”
  rule that would reject transformations adding Kings.
- Preserve useful existing enhancements and the Perkeo source-retention rule.
  Having a good target does not justify spending the last useful copying source
  when survival does not require it. Conversely, prefer developing surplus
  copies on the build's relevant cards when that investment is worthwhile.
- **Develop the deck even when a clearing play is already available.** In the user's
  explicit five-Kings/two-Queens example, use an available Strength on both
  Queens to create two more Kings when the Kings plan has a useful remaining
  horizon. The immediate clear probability can remain 100%; the benefit is
  better future draws and more consistent Four/Five of a Kind hands. Do not
  require a higher current score or a rescue condition to recommend this.
- Run this as a tiny, bounded strategic check before final clear publication,
  using the cached build profile and exact projected transformation. Recheck
  the known clearing play after the change so permanent progress does not
  accidentally sacrifice current survival through held-card/Joker effects.
  Value future participation against consumable scarcity and the extra action;
  do not simulate the whole remaining run or restart exhaustive hand search.
- For the positive regression, use a spare Strength if Perkeo is active, or a
  useful Strength without a generator-retention requirement, with more blinds
  to play. Recommend `use` with both Queen targets; one Execute click applies
  it, and the next fresh recommendation can play the already-clearing Kings.
  Preserve a sole useful Perkeo source unless a stated exception applies. If
  the current play completes the final required challenge boss, skip the
  development-only use because there is no remaining run to improve.
- Recommend an off-plan upgrade only for a concrete advantage, such as required
  immediate survival, a valuable held-card effect, conversion into the desired
  rank, or a supported boss-driven pivot. Explain that reason briefly rather
  than silently recommending an incidental Ace for a marginal score increase.
- Add a Kings-heavy Five of a Kind fixture with both a King and an Ace eligible
  for the same enhancement and no survival emergency: choose the King. Also
  test Queen-to-King Strength, harmful King-to-Ace Strength, correct Death
  direction, an explicit survival exception, and a mixed early deck that must
  not be incorrectly locked into a King strategy.
- Add the five-Kings/two-Queens guaranteed-clear fixture explicitly: recommend
  Strength despite zero immediate win-probability gain, keep total computation
  bounded, and publish the same use action to HUD/panel/Execute. Pair it with
  last-Perkeo-source and final-challenge-win cases that retain/play instead.
- Share the profile across consumable targets, pack choices, shop purchases
  and deck-development valuation to avoid contradictory recommendations. Cache
  it per relevant state so this common-sense preference does not add another
  expensive rollout to the fast path.

Relevant implementation entry points are `consumables.suggest`/`apply`,
`strategy.lua` hand/deck affinity and consumable/pack valuation, and snapshot
deck composition. Add regressions under `tests/advisor_consumables.lua`,
`tests/advisor_consumable_sequences.lua` and `tests/advisor_strategy.lua` as
appropriate. Specific feature coefficients remain heuristic until calibrated;
the reported build is a concrete acceptance case, not new win-rate evidence.

## 8. Simulations, calibration and evolutionary search

### 8.1 What exists versus what is missing

Implemented: a source loader, presentation-isolated original engine bootstrap,
experimental action dispatcher, bounded development wrappers, trace/provenance
records, formal seed registration and report auditing, and protocol tests.

Not implemented: a validated all-challenge full-run collector, persistent batched
episode workers, an evolutionary population, a versioned tuneable coefficient
vector, fitted probability calibration, and a measured time-to-completion policy
comparison. `benchmark.py init` registers/freezes a campaign; it does **not**
play 8,000 games. Do not describe its existence as completed simulation capacity.

### 8.2 Newly audited evaluation gaps — fix before product-level calibration

1. `benchmark.py` ~46 currently freezes `Advisor/*.lua`, `UI/advisor.lua` and
   `Core/Brainstorm.lua`, but omits such dependencies as `Core/challenge_opening.lua`,
   `UI/ui.lua`, `UI/challenge_opening.lua`, `lovely.toml` and the versioned native
   DLL. Extend the evaluated-product manifest according to actual behavior.
2. `runtime.lua` provides `A.opening = Brainstorm.ChallengeOpening` to the shared
   decision entry point. `engine_run.lua` currently loads ordering/boss/paid
   modules but **does not supply `modules.opening`**. Ordinary-start development
   remains useful, but the complete filtered-opening policy is not mirrored.
3. Formal registration assumes ordinary independent starts and excludes
   reroll-selected seeds. Filtered openings need a separate registered distribution
   schema: requested pair, search implementation/exception hashes, search limits,
   failures, setup procedure, and elapsed search time. Do not sneak selected
   seeds into an ordinary-start campaign.
4. The report audits win-rate gates, not actual duration/latency/skip/hand metrics
   or expected time to completion. Extend it before claiming the user's speed
   objective has been measured.
5. The adapter still uses a synthetic default unlock profile, explicitly marked
   `qualification_compatible=false`, digest
   `59a012422935e88481fa9fccd940d903e20aeb9f2c456f80fb28a2f4a57d7ddc`.
   Decide and document the intended unlock/discovery profile. Do not quietly read
   or modify the user's physical saves to make a benchmark look realistic.
6. Complete phase/terminal parity remains to be validated across challenge events,
   inventory/pack consumables, bosses, money, mutations and event scheduling.
   A parity review must inspect presentation shims for accidentally omitted rules.

`G.GAME.won` alone is not a trustworthy terminal indicator: original code can
set it before the final loss check. The adapter needs actual successful completion
handling, with GAME_OVER taking precedence. A partial run stopped by development
limits is neither a win nor a loss. Unsupported/timeouts/errors cannot disappear
from the denominator or become synthetic successful episodes.

### 8.3 Keep exact mechanics separate from uncertain preferences

**Exact layer:** legal actions, rank/suit/hand classification, score arithmetic,
RNG distributions, Joker increment/threshold/reset rules, boss/challenge effects,
cash costs, inventory limits, targeting and event order. Validate against source.

**Tuneable layer:** value of delayed growth, cash reserves, speculative synergy,
deck improvement, saved consumables, risk, future rounds, search allocation and
time. These values can be searched or calibrated, subject to constraints.

Start with a small vector, perhaps 10–30 bounded coefficients, extracted from
existing strategic constants. Examples: horizon discount; growth benefit weight;
purchase/interest penalty; reserve preference; synergy opportunity discount;
risk sensitivity; consumable retention; time cost of an extra hand or shop action.
These counts are a proposed starting scope, not tested requirements.

Initial default coefficients must reproduce current policy behavior. Record a
schema version and checksum alongside source hashes. A future possible file is
`Brainstorm/Advisor/policy_parameters.lua`; **it does not yet exist**. Keep physical
game-rule constants and legality guards outside the optimizer's parameter space.

### 8.4 Practical evolutionary experiment

The user's proposed “cells” can be candidate parameter vectors:

1. Preserve the current policy as an incumbent/control.
2. Generate perhaps 8–16 candidate vectors by small bounded perturbations.
   Use multiplicative/log-space changes for positive scale parameters when useful;
   maintain valid ranges and relationships. This is an engineering proposal,
   not a guaranteed best optimizer/population size.
3. Run candidates on the same development seed banks, stratified by challenge
   and ordinary versus filtered starts. Identical starting seeds reduce some
   noise; different actions can change subsequent RNG consumption, so trajectories
   are not assumed identical after policies diverge.
4. Use cheap mechanic/failure replays to reject invalid candidates, then a modest
   episode screen. Give surviving candidates more fresh development episodes
   rather than fully evaluating every weak variant.
5. Select promising candidates, preserve the incumbent and some diversity,
   mutate again, and log ancestry/configuration/evaluation budgets.
6. Test candidates on a separate validation bank that did not guide the mutations.
   Repeatedly selecting on that bank also risks overfitting; replace/reserve data
   as the experimental process grows.
7. Freeze the final candidate **before** generating formal qualification seeds.
   Qualification results must not feed further tuning on those same seeds.

A simple evolutionary strategy is a sensible first experiment because the
decision rules contain branches and discontinuities, and there may be only a
few dozen uncertain weights. It does not require a neural network, differentiable
simulator or GPU. Start CPU-first and measure where time actually goes. The
5090 may be useful later for a different learner, but is not automatically the
best way to accelerate branching Lua state transitions.

Do not let evolution exploit simulator mistakes. Source disagreement, illegal
actions, missing modeled transitions and hidden unsupported cases are bugs or
explicit coverage limitations, not rewards. Candidate quality must include
per-challenge competence/non-regression checks; a high aggregate reward can
otherwise sacrifice Jokerless or Fragile to improve easier decks.

### 8.5 Fitness and probability calibration are separate tasks

**Policy optimization** chooses coefficients that produce better actions.
**Probability calibration** asks whether a predicted 60% event happens about
60% of the time. A policy can improve without producing calibrated probabilities,
and calibrated forecasts alone do not guarantee optimal decisions.

Log predictions and outcomes at explicitly named horizons:

- Next play clears the current blind.
- Current resources can finish the remaining blind.
- This run wins the challenge.
- A future shop shows an affordable useful partner.

Do not compare one horizon's prediction with another horizon's outcome. For
probability models, evaluate reliability bins and proper probability errors on
held-out observations. Mean chip score is not itself a probability; a hand
with a mean above the target may still lose often. Counterfactual one-state
tests are useful for correctness, not population run-win calibration.

Early evolutionary fitness should prioritize real wins and explicit challenge
non-regression while collecting trustworthy timing. Ante reached or scoring
margin can screen developmental failures, but cannot become a substitute for
wins. With zero wins, time-to-success is unresolved/infinite; do not invent a
finite strong performance estimate from ante progress alone.

Once timing is credible, use the objective in §1. Headless throughput is an
engineering metric, not human playtime: the harness can omit or speed animations.
Model or measure actual game action costs and count user-click/decision waits,
opening search and retry setup. Keep successful and failed attempt durations.

### 8.6 Existing formal qualification protocol

The retained protocol defaults to 400 independent seeds per challenge, 8,000
episodes per attempt. It uses exact one-sided binomial lower bounds, with error
allowance `0.05 / (2 * 20 * attempt * (attempt + 1))` for each bound. A fresh
attempt number and fresh seeds are required after tuning. Stage 75 requires a
prior passing stage-50 report. Missing, unsupported, timed-out or erroneous
episodes prevent passing.

Required parity categories include `challenge_setup`, `draw_rng`, `hand_scoring`,
`joker_triggers`, `boss_rules`, `shop_and_packs`, `consumables`, `round_economy`,
`terminal_outcomes`, `verbatim_actions`, and `save_isolation`.

Episode records include ID, challenge, seed, manifest/policy/rules/adapter/trace
digests, source, exact-advice-following flag, decisions, outcome, ante and true
successful-completion flag. An adapter-validation record references the reviewed
parity report, hashes, challenge list and passed categories. There is no honest
validation attestation to manufacture at handoff.

This expensive formal campaign is not a prerequisite for every mechanics patch
or small development calibration experiment. The user previously preferred
lightweight checks, and has now proposed running simulations to calibrate.
Start with bounded development work and reliable instrumentation; do not launch
an unbounded 8,000-episode job just because a protocol exists.

### 8.7 Fast simulator, recurring failure analysis and performance loop

The user explicitly wants to investigate an efficient simulator to increase
success, discover common failure modes and analyze performance. This is a
development tool for the deterministic advisor as well as a possible basis
for evolutionary calibration; it does not require a neural policy or GPU
training. No new simulator or failure-analysis pipeline was built in this
documentation update.

Start from the existing source-engine harness and fix the fidelity/provenance
gaps above. Measure time spent in startup/reset, engine events, advice phases
and trace output before choosing a faster backend. Persistent isolated CPU
workers, bounded concurrency, compact records and cached immutable assets may
improve throughput. A native pure-state engine is a later option if profiling
justifies it, with differential checks against original game rules. Existing
Immolate seed search is not a full scoring/economy simulator. Never trade away
challenge/boss/consumable correctness for apparently higher win rates.

The proposed repeatable development loop is:

1. Run small, stratified seed batches for every challenge individually, with
   ordinary and filtered starts kept separate. Record policy/config/engine
   versions, seeds, complete outcomes and unsupported/error/timeout counts.
2. Keep compact decision features and predicted-versus-actual scores/resources;
   retain full replayable snapshots and action traces for failures, warnings
   and a representative success sample. Record run duration, hands, discards,
   skips, rerolls, inference latency, score calls and engine throughput.
3. Group recurring failures: insufficient scoring/scaling, missed boss or
   challenge constraints, poor upgrades/growth investment, economy mistakes,
   consumable waste/copy-pool dilution, preventable Glass/deck loss, unsafe
   discards, action errors and simulation/model mismatches. Record overlapping
   causes and uncertainty; the last losing hand alone may not be the cause.
4. Report frequency with its denominator per challenge/start cohort, where in
   the run it appears, likely severity, confidence and a small reproducible
   example. Keep engine errors separate from policy losses. A short incomplete
   rollout must not become a measured full-run win or loss.
5. Use targeted alternative-action replays to investigate causes and compare
   candidate fixes on matched development seeds. Different actions can change
   later RNG use, so do not imply identical subsequent trajectories. Rank work
   by observed prevalence, preventability, expected completion-time benefit
   and implementation cost; confirm improvements on separate validation seeds.

Report two different speed measures: simulation collection throughput and the
user's live recommendation latency/frame impact. Headless time omits real
animation/click costs; include an explicit cost model or actual measurements
when estimating challenge completion time. Reuse the §9 profiling corpus for
component timing, cancellations, allocations and p50/p95/p99 latency. Failure
replays should become regression fixtures and guide which missing mechanics
to implement before tuning uncertain weights.

Possible future entry points under `tools/advisor_eval` are a persistent
episode collector and a failure-report generator; neither exists at handoff.
Their first acceptance deliverable is a bounded, reproducible batch/report
that accounts for every attempted episode, correctly separates engine errors
from losses, can replay a flagged case, and measures startup/engine/advisor
costs separately. Compare incumbent and changed policies honestly before
claiming a win-rate or speed improvement. Large qualification campaigns remain
a separate optional stage, not a prerequisite for each feature installation.

## 9. Making recommendations faster and smoother

### Latest user requirement: stop on an obvious blind-clearing hand

**Requested fast path, not implemented.** The user explicitly prefers ending
the calculation as soon as a legal hand wins the current blind, except for
incredibly obvious, cheap conservation or strategic-development alternatives.
The later five-Kings/two-Queens clarification explicitly permits a useful
Strength transformation despite no immediate survival gain. Their Four of a
Kind build reportedly still incurred about 130,000 score comparisons. The
latest Death/Yorick examples also allow a bounded setup play or growth discard
when a comfortable position makes its future return worth the cost. Finding
a clearing hand does not justify maximizing overkill or exhausting the general
budget, and does not forbid these explicit short investment plans.

Implementation direction:

1. Test a small deterministic shortlist of obvious candidates **before** full
   subset enumeration: available rank groups, including Four/Five of a Kind,
   and candidates matching the build's actual hand levels/Jokers. Hand name
   alone does not establish a clear; score the legal selected cards under the
   current boss, debuffs, forced cards, held effects and order. Cheap candidate
   prioritization should make obvious builds cheap to recognize.
2. Stop expensive search as soon as a candidate is verified to cover the
   **remaining blind requirement**, including chips already earned. Apply the
   same stopping condition when a clear first appears during fallback search.
   An expected score above the target from Lucky/Bloodstone-style randomness
   is not a guaranteed clear: use deterministic covered scoring or a valid
   conservative lower bound, and retain the risk-aware fallback otherwise.
3. Permit only a tiny, explicit cap on cheap conservation/development
   alternatives after that clear: for example, a Level 1 clearing hand instead of degrading the
   main hand against The Arm, or a clearing subset that spares Glass/permanent
   debuffs, or Strength on two Queens to develop the Kings build while retaining
   a needed Perkeo source. Include a few bounded Death-target cycling or Yorick
   growth candidates when the investment allowance in §7 supports them. Use a
   cached build profile and revalidate completion after a projected change.
   Do not reopen general rollouts, broad
   consumable searches or permutation enumeration to marginally improve an
   already sufficient play. The cap must
   be fixed and visible in diagnostics, not an excuse for another full search.
4. Propagate the final clear through **`decision.run`**, skipping expensive
   discard/continuation, consumable, Joker-order, played-order and boss-sale
   passes. A return inside `search.run` alone cannot guarantee low total work.
   Keep the explicitly bounded development check before this final return;
   if it selects a valuable investment plan, publish its first concrete action
   (use, setup play or discard) instead of immediately taking the clear.
   Cheap alternatives may never spend scarce resources merely to increase
   surplus score. Lasting deck improvement can justify spending a consumable
   without increasing current win probability; preserve the user's
   time-to-completion objective and generation engine.
5. Publish one completed, stable action shared by HUD, panel and Execute, then
   end the worker. Do not keep calculating hidden replacements or present an
   early suggestion as provisional. Keep stale-state and legality validation.

Acceptance: construct an obvious deterministic Four of a Kind clearing fixture
with owned consumables and multiple Jokers, and verify the **total** decision
count stays within the shortlist/cheap-alternative bound. Spy on later passes
to ensure expensive specialist searches do not run; the explicitly bounded
conservation/development check is allowed. Also cover an obvious Five of a Kind, accumulated
chips, boss restrictions, probabilistic false clears, stale state, Arm/Glass
conservation, strategic Strength/Death/Yorick examples and absence of a clear.
Non-clearing hands still need the existing deeper search. The exact reported
live state has not been captured; the roughly
130,000 figure is a user observation, not a reproduced benchmark.

This is an intentional policy change favoring sufficiently good immediate
clears and latency; it need not select the global maximum-scoring hand or match
the old exhaustive policy's action. Validate legality, reliable clearing,
bounded conservation/development behavior and end-to-end latency rather than
demanding identical overkill choices.

### Explicit Glass conservation preference

The user specifically wants to avoid playing Glass cards unnecessarily because
they may break. When comparable reliable clears are available, prefer the one
with less expected permanent loss, especially when a Glass card is central to
the rank build, has other valuable properties or is a valuable copying source.
Extra overkill alone cannot justify risking that card. Preserve it in hand
when the clearing play does not need it.

Include a small Glass-saving candidate check in the fast path above; a first
clearing candidate that uses Glass must not prevent this obvious alternative.
Apply the same loss valuation to Death-target cycling and other development
plays: being comfortably ahead does not make destruction free. Use actual
breakage eligibility/probability, Joker effects and challenge rules, including
Fragile, rather than assuming all Glass exposures have the same risk.

This is a conditional preference: use Glass when needed for survival or when
a concrete strategic benefit justifies the expected loss. Do not turn it into
a ban that rejects the only clearing hand. Current `search.lua:better`
conserves card count among clears but has no Glass-specific tie-break;
`ordering.lua:future_metrics` has a partial Glass penalty using a fixed 0.25
heuristic. `scoring.after_play` still rejects scored-Glass continuation because
destruction is not simulated. The new requirement must cover the whole decision
and actual modifiers; this documentation update does not implement that work.

Regression requirements: two reliable clears with/without a valuable Glass
card choose the preserving play within the tiny check budget; a sole clearing
Glass play remains allowed; setup plans include breakage costs; and remaining
deck value matters when deciding which Glass card to risk. Validate actual
selection/destruction rules in the source rather than relying on a warning
label. Relevant files are `search.lua`, `ordering.lua`, `hand_ordering.lua`,
`scoring.lua` and `decision.lua`; use the existing cycling/resource/ordering
fixtures and `tests/advisor_decision_integration.lua` for end-to-end coverage.

### Measure the right quantities first

Add an opt-in profiling mode and a saved-snapshot corpus. Proposed files might be
`tools/advisor_eval/profile_decisions.py` and a versioned corpus directory;
**neither exists yet**. Profile:

- End-to-end settled-state-to-final-recommendation p50/p95/p99 latency.
- Snapshot capture, fingerprinting, each decision pass and publication separately.
- Longest uninterrupted batch and game-frame impact.
