- Score calls per second, allocations/GC, cache hit rate and memory use.
- Cancellation/restart frequency and work discarded because the state changed.
- Unsupported/truncated rates and whether shop scoring falls back as a whole.
- Decision/action agreement with the reference implementation.

Use representative ordinary hands, growth/retrigger/copy rows, large hands,
complex shops, bosses and every challenge's special constraints. Do not tune
performance only on an empty-Joker eight-card hand. Set latency targets after
measuring a baseline; no latency target or speedup has been demonstrated yet.

### Further optimization order after the obvious-clear fast path

1. **Capture and fingerprint costs.** They recur every working frame and are
   outside the 4 ms coroutine budget. Cache immutable portions or introduce a
   cheaper change signature only after proving that every decision-relevant
   card, order, counter, cash, level, boss, deck and inventory change invalidates
   the result. Keep the final live-state execution check. UI animation/hover
   noise should not invalidate scoring.
2. **Prepared data within one snapshot.** Reuse Joker identities/flags and copy
   structure, card rank/suit masks, legal subset arrays and hand classification
   where genuinely invariant. Different consumables, debuffs, hand sizes,
   enhancements or condition-changing Jokers require new keys. Copy-target order
   is not invariant under reordering.
3. **Reduce allocation and repeated scores.** Bounded per-decision memoization,
   reusable scratch structures and less table cloning may help. Keep candidate
   writable state isolated; a speed optimization must not leak growth from one
   candidate into another. Preserve card identities and deterministic ties.
4. **Skip provably irrelevant passes.** Avoid permutations when no supported
   order-sensitive effect exists. Do not use broad shortcuts that miss held
   effects, Blueprint/Brainstorm, editions, retriggers or future growth.
   Use valid bounds for pruning; a heuristic score is not a safe upper bound.
5. **Allocate search intelligently.** After a correct fast baseline, investigate
   additional matched samples for close comparisons and early stopping when
   alternatives are clearly inferior. Preserve completed paired rounds and avoid
   evaluation-order bias. Changing budgets/shortlists changes policy behavior
   and needs quality evaluation, not just a stopwatch.
6. **Consider native code only after profiling.** A pure scorer/classifier kernel
   or detached worker could help if it dominates. Differential-test it against
   Lua, retain a fallback and version its ABI. Preserve arithmetic/RNG precision.
   Native/GPU work is not inherently faster for tiny, branch-heavy jobs.

Do not “fix” speed by publishing an actionable provisional suggestion and then
silently replacing it after the user opens the panel. That caused a previous
user-visible bug. A completed, stable recommendation is part of correctness.

The simulation collector has a separate optimization path: persistent isolated
workers, batched jobs, compact observations and concise traces reduce process,
serialization and I/O overhead. Measure engine reset/step time independently
from teacher search. A twenty-second source episode that runs tens of thousands
of scores per decision says little about raw simulator step throughput.

Native Immolate builds deliberately use `-ffp-contract=off` and `-fno-fast-math`
for Lua-equivalent seed behavior. Do not enable fast-math or FP8/FP4 for exact
game arithmetic to chase throughput. Low-precision neural training, if ever
resumed, is a separate experiment and does not make these rules approximate.

## 10. Clear to-do list and suggested work sequence

Items below are **not completed** unless checked. Keep this section updated as
work lands. The next chat should execute bounded useful slices, not rewrite the
whole advisor before delivering another improvement.

### A. First implementation slice: latest counterexamples, then progression

- [ ] Implement the obvious-clearing-hand shortlist and immediate stop in §9,
  including a tiny cheap-conservation/development allowance and an end-to-end
  decision exit.
- [ ] Add a Four/Five of a Kind regression that checks total score calls and
  skipped specialist passes, not only the selected hand's score.
- [ ] Enforce the explicit Glass-conservation preference in §9 across the fast
  path and setup plans; test a preserving clear and a necessary-Glass clear.
- [ ] Protect the last useful Perkeo consumable source in §7, including Strength
  rank-development value, surplus copies and explicit survival/final-win cases.
- [ ] Apply retention consistently to consumption, sales and short sequences;
  do not let a later specialist undo a preserved-source decision.
- [ ] Generalize Perkeo source retention into marginal whole-inventory value:
  copying odds, foregone better copies, Negative accumulation, sequential copy
  events and feasible buy/use/sell sequences at the actual shop-exit horizon.
- [ ] Integrate matching Planet/Observatory held value versus permanent levels
  and alternative copying engines. Add the dilution and nonlinear-multiplier
  cases in §7; avoid a Strength/Hierophant-specific rule.
- [ ] Add a shared inexpensive build profile and future participation value for
  upgrade targets; prefer Kings in the reported Kings-heavy Five of a Kind
  case, with explicit reasons for off-plan exceptions.
- [ ] Test enhancement targeting separately from rank transformations: Queen
  to King, King to Ace and Death copying direction; preserve Perkeo source value.
- [ ] Permit useful development despite an already-guaranteed clear: five Kings
  plus two Queens should prompt spare Strength on the Queens. Keep the check
  bounded, revalidate the known clear, and test Perkeo/final-win exceptions.

- [ ] Add a shared pure discard transition; migrate Green/Ramen with identical
  outputs before adding new effects.
- [ ] Implement exact Yorick per-card counter/growth from live fields, respecting
  debuff/Blueprint semantics and source callback order.
- [ ] Implement Burnt's exact first-discard level transition, actual category
  and copy behavior, then a dynamic build-aware choice of which hand to upgrade.
  Include purchase value, affordability/survival, and choice-switching fixtures.
- [ ] Use it in one-discard sampling and continuation; remove Yorick's blocker
  only after all affected paths pass source-parity and behavior fixtures.
- [ ] Add explicit Yorick/scaler strategic features so a fresh searched Legendary
  is not rated as an unexplained default Joker. Future-value weights remain
  explicitly heuristic until calibrated.
- [ ] Add the state-dependent investment allowance in §7: comfortable early
  positions can spend more spare hands/discards for positive future value;
  current income and fastest blind clear cannot be absolute priorities.
- [ ] Add bounded non-clearing play/draw/Death plans and larger Yorick growth
  discards, with exact transitions, completion reserves, real economy costs
  and stopping rules that cannot reset indefinitely after each action.
- [ ] Test matched strong/fragile states, target absent/already found, setup
  plays that accidentally clear, paid discards and final-win/no-payoff cases.
- [ ] Instrument snapshot/hash/pass/total latency and score-call counts on a
  saved, deterministic fixture corpus; establish a baseline before optimizing.
- [ ] Preserve legality, reliable-clear and resource-conservation guarantees;
  revise any blanket clear-suppression test to allow the explicitly bounded,
  worthwhile investment cases while keeping the Perkeo-source guard.

### B. Simulation/calibration prerequisites, parallel to A

- [ ] Complete evaluated-file hashing/freezing, including relevant opening module,
  UI/patch/native/config dependencies; distinguish policy from user settings.

### C. Next strategic features, ranked in the preceding conversation

The estimates are **speculative percentage-point gains on affected runs**, not
measured patch improvements, delivery commitments or values to add together.
The original rank put urgent irreversible events first. The latest specific
obvious-clear, Perkeo and Kings-targeting feedback now leads slice A, followed
by the earlier Yorick/calibration work, without discarding these broader
priorities.

| Prior rank | Work | Why / bounded starting point | Earlier gain guess |
|---|---|---|---:|
| 1 | Executable preparation for irreversible challenge events | Dagger placement/sacrifice before selecting a blind; Typecast final-row preparation before Ante 4 lock | +5–15 pp |
| 2 | Discard growth and effects | Yorick/Castle first, then Burnt/Mail/Faceless/Hit the Road, Trading Card and Purple generation | +4–12 pp |
| 3 | Scoring distributions and destruction | Start with Fragile's certain Glass loss; model future deck size, then stochastic Glass/Hook/Crimson cases, deterministic DNA population changes and score risk | +3–10 pp |
| 4 | Consumables for long-term deck development | Hanged Man, copying, destruction, seals and Spectrals; Canio/Perkeo plans and Jokerless deck quality | +4–12 pp |
| 5 | Unified short action sequences | Bounded search over discard/use/reorder/play combinations, rather than independently overwriting proposals | +4–12 pp |
| 6 | Upcoming-blind/boss-aware purchases and skips | Compare actual next-blind vulnerability, income/growth forgone and real time saved | +3–8 pp plus possible speed benefit |

Additional concrete outstanding challenge work:

- [ ] **On a Knife's Edge:** preserve valuable Jokers adjacent to the pinned
  Dagger; model sacrifice and growth before the blind starts. Warning text is
  not an executable safeguard.
- [ ] **Typecast:** build and commit the eventual eternal row before its lock,

- [ ] **Fragile:** track certain Glass destruction, usable cards remaining and

- [ ] **Monolith:** establish and preserve the most-played-hand lead and plan a

- [ ] **Jokerless:** reliable deck shaping/levels/seals and boss survival. The

- [ ] **Golden Needle:** single-hand risk and beneficial discard triggers;

- [ ] **Double or Nothing:** extend existing per-play permanent debuffs into

- [ ] **Omelette / searched Legendaries:** plan real engine development after
  acquiring the opening, including economy and progression conditions.

## 11. Commands, tests and reproduction details

All commands below run in PowerShell from the repository. They are reference
commands, not instructions to automatically launch every campaign during resume.

```powershell
Set-Location -LiteralPath 'C:\Users\trevo\Documents\GitHub\Brainstorm-Rerolled-Rerolled'
git status --short
python tests/run_lua_tests.py
python -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_advisor_benchmark.py
git diff --check
```

The Lua runner loads the installed `lua51.dll` through ctypes; it does not run
Balatro.exe. It can run a focused set:

```powershell
python tests/run_lua_tests.py tests/advisor_play_state.lua tests/advisor_resource_decisions.lua tests/advisor_search.lua
python tests/run_lua_tests.py tests/advisor_runtime.lua tests/advisor_execution.lua tests/advisor_decision_integration.lua
```

- Opening integration: `advisor_challenge_opening`, `advisor_challenge_opening_ui`.

Append `.lua` to each fixture name. The syntax fixture currently enumerates 21
runtime Lua files; add new modules to it. The runtime's loader and the engine
module bundle also need wiring when introducing policy modules.

### Bounded original-source development

```powershell
python tools/advisor_eval/engine_probe.py --challenge c_omelette_1 --seed ADVISOR1
python tools/advisor_eval/engine_probe_development.py --seeds ADVISOR7 --challenge c_omelette_1 --timeout 45 --label development
```

The development wrapper explicitly uses hidden subprocesses and per-episode
timeouts. `ADVISOR*` seeds are development data, never untouched qualification
seeds. Avoid overwriting a useful old trace: choose a distinct alphanumeric label.

For a strict action limit plus a hard wall-clock limit, the following wrapper
uses `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`; it still only runs the Python source-engine probe:

```powershell
@'
import pathlib, subprocess, sys
log = pathlib.Path('tools/advisor_eval/engine_probe_next_development.log')
with log.open('w', encoding='utf-8') as output:
    subprocess.run([
        sys.executable, 'tools/advisor_eval/engine_probe.py', '--episode',
        '--challenge', 'c_omelette_1', '--seed', 'ADVISOR7',
        '--stop-after-step', '20'
    ], stdout=output, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, timeout=55,
       creationflags=subprocess.CREATE_NO_WINDOW, check=True)
print(log.resolve())
'@ | python -
```

Do not create a visible QA/game window to obtain these results. Do not silently
convert a timeout or development action limit into a terminal outcome. Inspect
concise JSON summaries before opening full debug snapshots.

### Native opening tests/build

```powershell
cmake --build Immolate/build-v2.16-challenge-portable --target Immolate brainstorm_challenge_opening_regression
ctest --test-dir Immolate/build-v2.16-challenge-portable -R 'brainstorm_(challenge_opening|api|soul_scanner)_regression' --output-on-failure
python Immolate/tests/source_challenge_opening_probe.py
```

The existing build cache is Release/Ninja, GCC from mingw-winlibs, portable
native optimizations OFF, PGO OFF, tests ON. If it is missing, inspect
`Immolate/CMakeLists.txt` and use a new isolated build directory. Do not overwrite
a loaded installed DLL. The source probe accepts `--install` and `--native`
paths when testing a different installation/build. It covers opening mechanics,
not win rates. The normal API and Soul tests protect existing reroll behavior.

### Formal registration/audit — future, after prerequisites

```powershell
python tools/advisor_eval/benchmark.py init tools/advisor_eval/runs/stage50-attempt1 --engine 'C:/Program Files (x86)/Steam/steamapps/common/Balatro/Balatro.exe' --stage 50 --attempt 1
python tools/advisor_eval/benchmark.py report tools/advisor_eval/runs/stage50-attempt1 --validation tools/advisor_eval/runs/adapter-validation.json
```

These are examples from the existing protocol, not evidence that attempt 1 or
the validation file exists. `init` does not execute episodes. Audit the campaign
directory/attempt history before selecting an attempt number. Registration and

described in §8. Do not manufacture the adapter-validation JSON to make a report
pass. `training_allowed=false` in the old report schema concerns the deferred

## 12. Installation workflow and rollback information

**Current user instruction:** install each feature after successful
implementation and appropriate verification. The previous estimated ten-point
batching threshold no longer applies. This docs-only update implements no
runtime feature and therefore does not itself trigger a mod deployment.

Installed v2.16 files are backed up under:

`C:/Users/trevo/AppData/Roaming/Balatro/Mods/Brainstorm/deployment-backups/advisor-20260910-150227`

The `deployment.json` manifest stores every old/new hash. New files have
`before: null`; do not assume the backup contains a previous version of them.
The installed legacy `Immolate.dll` was preserved, and the Core loader prefers
`Immolate-v2.16.dll` on Windows when present. The three required C++ runtime DLLs
matched existing copies, so dependency replacement was unnecessary.

The script
[install_advisor.ps1](C:/Users/trevo/Documents/GitHub/Brainstorm-Rerolled-Rerolled/tools/install_advisor.ps1)
backs up selected runtime files before copying, checks installed hashes and
checks config before/after. It writes the Core entrypoint last and never touches
saves or starts/stops a game. It is currently **version-specific**: both the
manifest version and native filename are hardcoded for 2.16. Update or parameterize
those fields before shipping a later version. Do not relabel an old DLL/version
or overwrite a DLL that may be loaded by the user's running game.

For an actual next release:

1. Finish and test the intended feature slice; inspect relevant regressions and
   source parity. Source changes should be concrete before any final deployment.
2. Update Core/compat versions and the installer/dependency list as needed.
3. Build native changes in isolation. If the installed binary may be loaded,
   deploy a new versioned sidecar and point the next-load path to it.
4. Re-read the current config hash; preserve that file, not a historical copy.
5. Back up, copy, verify all selected runtime files and record the manifest.
6. Update estimates/evidence honestly and state that activation waits for the
   user's next normal restart. Do not restart/fullscreen the game for them.

A rollback, if needed, must use the matching manifest and account for files
created by that release. It should be deliberate and scoped to the mod; do not
run recursive cleanup or restore the user's old settings indiscriminately.

the advisor, not estimates of the user's current live run.

The filtered column assumes a useful compatible pair, not arbitrary Any/Any

opening-distribution advantage on several challenges; the ordinary policy's

and not claim another opening boost from the same existing feature.

probes in §6. Neither 50% nor 75% has been established for any challenge by the
formal protocol. Estimates should eventually be replaced by held-out rates
with uncertainty and reported duration, not kept as permanently guessed scores.

## 14. Previous model/repository research — deferred context

The user earlier explored a GPU-trained model and reuse of Balatron, then
explicitly returned to deterministic development. A read-only investigation is
saved at
[BALATRON_REUSE_REVIEW.md](C:/Users/trevo/Documents/GitHub/Brainstorm-Rerolled-Rerolled/tools/advisor_eval/BALATRON_REUSE_REVIEW.md).

That review inspected Balatron commit
`f91a948bb6075390a361d9d7e37c4ff4c42ceb3d` and discussed an alternative native
simulator project. It is a historical source review, not a current-upstream
audit, replicated training result or imported implementation. No upstream code,
checkpoint, learner, dependency stack or GPU workload was incorporated here.
Read it only if revisiting that direction; do not repeat the whole internet
investigation just to improve a Lua heuristic.

The durable lessons were: experience collection and simulator fidelity may
dominate training time; normal Red Deck/White Stake encoders/actions do not
automatically represent challenge rules; a fast opening-seed predicate is not a
fast complete gameplay simulator; and hardware precision tricks do not repair
missing mechanics or poor rewards. Immolate can contribute RNG/pool-generation
pieces, but its normal search timeline assumes selected game progress rather
than performing complete hand scoring and economic transitions.

Any earlier GPU-hour numbers in that document are conditional planning
arithmetic, not a measured budget to achieve 75% on this 5090. The present
evolutionary coefficient-tuning idea can be investigated without that neural
training investment. Deterministic inference and offline optimized preferences
are compatible.
